\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}\text{-}\bf{Alg_{ps}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{P}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\text{-}\bf{Alg}_{ps}}

Corollary 6.25
.

The functor
\mathbb{R}_{G}\colon{\mathcal{O}}\text{-}{\bf Alg_{ps}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\text{-}\bf{Alg}_{ps}
extends to a symmetric multifunctor.

7.
From
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-algebras to
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras

We recall the notion of an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad in
G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
and in
\mathbf{Cat}(G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}})
in
Section 7.1
. For a category of operators
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
arising from a chaotic
E_{\infty}

G
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}
, we produce a pseudofunctor
\zeta_{G}\colon\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.62689pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.62689pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.62689pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}{\hbox{\kern 31.62689pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
that is a section to
\xi_{G}
in
Proposition 7.4
.
Finally, in
Theorem 7.13
, we show that pulling back along
\zeta_{G}
defines a symmetric multifunctor from
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-algebras to
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras.

Throughout this section, we restrict attention to the topological case
\mathcal{V}=\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
and thus
G\mathcal{V}=G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
.

7.1.
E_{\infty}

G
-operads

So far, our chaotic operads have been quite general. We now restrict attention to a chaotic
E_{\infty}

G
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}
of
G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
-categories and its associated category of operators
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}={\mathscr{D}}_{G}({\mathcal{O}})
over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
.

Definition 7.1
.

An operad
{\mathcal{O}}
in
G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
is an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad
if for all
n\geq 0
and all subgroups
\Lambda
of
G\times\Sigma_{n}
, the fixed point space
{\mathcal{O}}(n)^{\Lambda}
is contractible if
\Lambda\cap\Sigma_{n}=\{e\}
and is empty otherwise.

An operad
{\mathcal{O}}
in
\mathbf{Cat}(G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}})
is an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad
if the operad
B{\mathcal{O}}
obtained by applying the classifying space functor levelwise is an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad of
G
-spaces.

The condition on fixed-points implies that for an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad in
G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}}
, the space
{\mathcal{O}}(n)
is a universal principal
(G,\Sigma_{n})
-bundle. Algebras over
E_{\infty}

G
-operads, are, up to group completion, equivariant infinite loop spaces with deloopings with respect to all finite-dimensional
G
-representations, and thus give rise to genuine
G
-spectra. For more background and examples we refer the reader to
[
13
, Section 2.1]
.

The following result shows how chaotic categories are useful in this context.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be a chaotic operad in
\mathbf{Cat}(G\mathbf{\mathcal{U}})
. Then
{\mathcal{O}}
is an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad if and only if for all
n\geq 0
and all subgroups
\Lambda
of
G\times\Sigma_{n}
, the fixed point object space
(\mathbf{Ob}{\mathcal{O}}(n))^{\Lambda}
is non-empty if
\Lambda\cap\Sigma_{n}=\{e\}
and is empty otherwise.

Proof.

As noted in
[
16
, Remark 1.15]
, the classifying space of a non-empty chaotic
\mathcal{U}
-category is contractible. Thus, the statement follows by noting that if
{\mathcal{O}}
is chaotic, then
{\mathcal{O}}(n)^{\Lambda}
is also chaotic.
∎

7.2.
The section map
\zeta_{G}
from
\mathscr{F}_{G}
to
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}

Recall that
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
comes equipped with functors

\iota_{G}\colon\Pi_{G}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \xi_{G}\colon{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{G}

such that
\xi_{G}\circ\iota_{G}
is the inclusion. We here define an (equivariant) section

\textstyle{\zeta_{G}\colon\mathscr{F}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}}

to
\xi_{G}
.

Definition 7.3
.

A
pseudomorphism

\textstyle{\nu\colon{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}_{G}}
of
\mathbf{CO}
s over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
is a
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-pseudofunctor over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
and under
\Pi_{G}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be a chaotic
E_{\infty}

G
-operad in
\mathbf{Cat}(G\mathcal{U})
and let
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}={\mathscr{D}}_{G}({\mathcal{O}})
.
Then there exists a pseudomorphism

\textstyle{\zeta_{G}\colon\mathscr{F}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}}

of
\mathbf{CO}
s over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
and an invertible
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-pseudotransformation

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\chi}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}.}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{G}}

Proof.

On objects, we have no choice:
\zeta_{G}
is the identity. More generally, on
\Pi_{G}
we must take
\zeta_{G}=\iota_{G}
. Given finite
G
-sets

\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{n}^{\beta}
, we must specify a based
G
-equivariant function

\zeta_{G}\colon\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ob}{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta}).

To define an equivariant function, it suffices to specify the function on each
G
-orbit. Moreover, an equivariant function out of an orbit is completely determined by its value at any point in the orbit. We thus
choose, for each
\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{n}^{\beta}
, a point of each
G
-orbit of the
G
-set
\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})
.

Let
f\in\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})
be such a chosen element. Let
H\leq G
be the stabilizer of
f
. The section
\zeta_{G}
must send
f
to an
H
-fixed object of the
G
-category
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})
that is in the component
\prod_{1\leq j\leq k}{\mathcal{O}}(f^{-1}(j))
of
f
. Since
{\mathcal{O}}
is an
E_{\infty}

G
-operad, the
H
-fixed point subset of this component has contractible classifying space, by
[
39
, Theorem 5.4]
, hence is nonempty. Thus we can choose an
H
-fixed object
\zeta_{G}(f)
in the component of
f
. The only exception to such use of choices is that we already know the definition of
\zeta_{G}
on
\Pi_{G}
, so these choices only apply to morphisms of
\mathscr{F}_{G}
that are not in
\Pi_{G}
.

The claim is that these choices specify an equivariant pseudofunctor
\zeta_{G}
. The equivariance has been forced by the definition of
\zeta_{G}
: if
f
is one of our distinguished points, then
\zeta_{G}(g\cdot f)=g\cdot\zeta_{G}(f)
. To see the pseudofunctor structure, we must specify a
G
-equivariant natural isomorphism

\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{n}^{\beta},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})\wedge\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{G}\wedge\zeta_{G}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\varphi}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{n}^{\beta},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})\wedge{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ}
\textstyle{\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{G}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma}).}

The component of
\varphi
at
(h,f)
must be a morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})
of the form

\varphi_{h,f}\colon\zeta_{G}(h)\circ\zeta_{G}(f)\longrightarrow\zeta_{G}(h\circ f).

Since both of these points in
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})
live over
h\circ f\in\mathscr{F}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{p}^{\gamma})
and
{\mathcal{O}}
is chaotic, there is a unique morphism with the required source and target.

We claim that
\varphi
is
G
-equivariant. This means that
\varphi_{g\cdot h,g\cdot f}=g\cdot\varphi_{h,f}
for
g\in G
. This again holds because
{\mathcal{O}}
is chaotic: there is a unique morphism between any two objects, so these two morphisms are necessarily the same. The compatibility of
\varphi
with triple composition follows again from the uniqueness of these morphisms.

For an object
\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}
, the 1-cell component
\chi_{\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}}\colon\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}\longrightarrow\zeta_{G}\circ\xi_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha})=\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}
is the identity map. We need to construct the pseudonaturality constraint, which is an invertible
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-transformation

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\zeta_{G}\circ\xi_{G}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\chi}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\chi_{\mathbf{m}^{\alpha}})^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\chi_{\mathbf{n}^{\beta}})_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta}).}

For a
1
-cell
d\in{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(\mathbf{m}^{\alpha},\mathbf{n}^{\beta})
, the object
\zeta_{G}\circ\xi_{G}(d)
is in the same component of the same
G
-fixed point subset as
d
, hence there is a unique morphism
\chi_{d}\colon\zeta_{G}\circ\xi_{G}(d)\longrightarrow d
, and this assignment is continuous and equivariant. The uniqueness implies naturality and the required compatibility with
\varphi
. That and the evident inverse isomorphism lead to the conclusion.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

Although the section
\zeta_{G}
is strictly functorial when restricted to
\Pi_{G}
, it is not when only one of the morphisms of a composite in
\mathscr{F}_{G}
is in
\Pi_{G}
, even when
G=e
. Let
p_{n}\colon\mathbf{n}\longrightarrow\bf{1}
be the based function that sends
j
to
1
for
1\leq j\leq n
. Then
\zeta(p_{n})=(p_{n};d)
for some
d\in{\mathcal{O}}(n)
. For a permutation
\sigma\in\Sigma_{n}
, we have
p_{n}\circ\sigma=p_{n}
, while
\zeta(p_{n})\circ\zeta(\sigma)=(p_{n};d\cdot\sigma)
. In the cases of interest
\Sigma_{n}
acts freely on
{\mathcal{O}}(n)
and we cannot have
\zeta(p_{n})\circ\zeta(\sigma)=\zeta(p_{n})
.
It is this fact that led us to the distinctions highlighted in

Remark 6.18
.

Recall the definition of a map between pseudo-commutative categories of operators over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
from
Definitions
5.11
and
6.13
. We adapt that definition to pseudomorphisms of
\mathbf{CO}
s over
\mathscr{F}_{G}
.

Definition 7.6
.

A
pseudomorphism of pseudo-commutative
\mathbf{CO}
s over
\mathscr{F}_{G}

consists of a pseudomorphism
\textstyle{\nu\colon{\mathscr{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}}

of
\mathbf{CO}
s over
\mathscr{F}_{G}

and a
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-pseudotransformation

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}\wedge{\mathscr{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu\wedge\nu}
\scriptstyle{\circledast_{\mathscr{D}}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mu}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}\wedge\mathscr{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circledast_{\mathscr{E}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}}

satisfying analogues of all the axioms in
Definition 5.11
.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}
be a chaotic
G
-operad and let
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}={\mathscr{D}}_{G}({\mathcal{O}})
.
Then the section

\textstyle{\zeta_{G}\colon\mathscr{F}_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}_{G}}

is a pseudomorphism of pseudo-commutative
\mathbf{CO}
s.

Proof.

We must produce a
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-pseudotransformation
\mu
as above.
Since
\zeta_{G}
is the identity on objects and
\mu
must restrict to the identity on
\Pi_{G}
, we take the
1
-cell component of
\mu
to be the identity. Given
1
-cells
f
and
h
in
\mathscr{F}_{G}
, we must produce an invertible two-cell

\mu_{f,h}\colon\zeta_{G}(f\wedge h)\cong\zeta_{G}(f)\circledast\zeta_{G}(h),

that is the component of a
G\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
-transformation.
Since the specified source and target live over
f\wedge h
in
\mathscr{F}_{G}
, the fact that
{\mathcal{O}}
is chaotic implies that there is a unique choice for
\mu_{f,h}
; that
\mu
is natural and equivariant, and satisfies all the axioms follows for the same reason.
∎

7.3.
The symmetric multifunctor
\zeta_{G}^{*}

We have elected to work with (strict)
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-algebras but, due to
Remark 7.5
, when we precompose with our section
\zeta_{G}
, we only produce weak

\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras; recall from
Notation 4.27
that we defined the objects and morphisms of
\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}

as enriched pseudofunctors from
\mathscr{F}_{G}
to
\underline{\mathbf{Cat}}(G\mathcal{V}_{\ast})
and pseudotransformations, with no strictness conditions over
\Pi_{G}
. The following proposition follows immediately from the definitions.

Proposition 7.8
.

Pullback along the section
\zeta_{G}
defines a 2-functor

\zeta_{G}^{*}\colon\!{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\text{-}\bf{Alg}_{ps}\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}.

Remark 7.9
.

Since
\zeta_{G}
restricts to the inclusion
\iota_{G}
on
\Pi_{G}
, for any
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-algebra
{\mathcal{X}}
, the underlying
\Pi_{G}
-algebra of
\zeta_{G}^{*}{\mathcal{X}}
is the underlying
\Pi_{G}
-algebra of
{\mathcal{X}}
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-algebra. Then the pseudonatural isomorphism
\chi
of
Proposition 7.4
induces an invertible pseudomorphism

\chi\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\cong\xi_{G}^{*}\zeta_{G}^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}

of weak
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras (see
Definition 4.23
) whose components are identity maps.

We define multimorphisms of weak
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras following
Definition 6.19
, but deleting its strictness conditions with respect to
\Pi_{G}
. Note that
\circledast
is just
\wedge
on
\mathscr{F}_{G}
. Recall that we are working in
\mathbf{Cat}(G\mathcal{U}_{\ast})
in this section.

Definition 7.11
.

We define a (symmetric) multicategory
\mathbf{Mult}(\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg})
of (weak)
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras whose underlying category is
\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}
, as follows.
For weak
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras
{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq k
, and
{\mathcal{Y}}
, a
k
-ary morphism

\underline{{\mathcal{X}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}
consists of a
\mathscr{F}_{G}^{\wedge k}
-pseudomorphism

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 28.64066pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.64066pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F\colon{\mathcal{X}}_{1}\overline{\wedge}\dots\overline{\wedge}{\mathcal{X}}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.64066pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}{\hbox{\kern 52.64066pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\wedge^{k}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Composition and the symmetric group action are given by the corresponding pasting diagrams, as done explicitly in
Definition 5.15
.

The unpacking of this definition is similar to the unpackings given for Definitions
5.15
and
6.19
, with the caveat that the coherence diagrams in
Section 11.3
must account for the pseudofunctoriality constraints of the weak
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras.

Remark 7.12
.

We could define an analogous multicategory
\mathscr{F}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}
of weak structures, but we would not have a
prolongation multifunctor
\mathbb{P}\colon\mathscr{F}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}
since we would no longer have the compatibility with
\Pi

that we used in the proof of
Theorem 6.23
. However, naturally occurring examples of
\mathscr{F}
-pseudoalgebras that do not arise from use of the section
often do have such compatibility with
\Pi
and thus can be prolonged to
\mathscr{F}_{G}
-pseudoalgebras.

We have the following theorem, whose proof is essentially the same as that of
Theorem 5.20
. Recall
Notation 4.28
.

Theorem 7.13
.

Pullback along
\zeta_{G}
induces a symmetric multifunctor

\zeta_{G}^{*}\colon\mathbf{Mult}({\mathscr{D}}_{G})\longrightarrow\mathbf{Mult}(\mathscr{F}_{G}\text{-}{\bf PsAlg}).

8.
Strictification of pseudoalgebras

For clarity about what is general and what is special and also for simplicity of notation, we revert to a general
\mathcal{V}
satisfying our standard assumptions in this section. The reader may prefer to focus on
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{U}
or
\mathcal{V}=G\mathcal{U}
, but equivariance and topology play no role in this section.

8.1.
Power-Lack strictification

We here specialize a general result of Power and Lack
[
20
,
42
]
(see also
[
16
, Theorem 0.1]
)
about strictification of pseudoalgebras over a 2-monad. Let
{\eus{C}}
be a
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-category, and recall the 2-categories
{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{Alg}}
and
{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}
(
Notation 4.27
) of
{\eus{C}}
-algebras and weak
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebras. We prove the existence of a strictification 2-functor
\mathrm{St}\colon{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{Alg}}
.
While the general result specializes to give everything we need,
we prefer to give an independent account that translates out from the theory of
2
-monads and includes an explicit construction. We are implicitly
using the existence of an enhanced factorization system on
\mathbf{Cat}(\mathcal{V}_{\ast})

[
16
, §4]
to apply Power and Lack’s result to our context.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
{\eus{C}}
be a
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-category.
The inclusion of
2
-categories

\mathbb{J}\colon{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{Alg}}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}

has a left 2-adjoint

\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}}\colon{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{Alg}}.

The component of the unit
i
of the
2
-adjunction is a
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphism which is a levelwise equivalence (
Definition 4.25
).

For brevity, we will omit the subscript
{\eus{C}}
and simply write
\mathrm{St}
for the strictification functor when the category
{\eus{C}}
is clear from the context.

Proof.

For a weak
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra
({\mathcal{X}},\theta,\varphi)
, we define the (strict)
{\eus{C}}
-algebra
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}
as follows.

For
c
in
{\eus{C}}
, the
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-category
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c)
has
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-object of objects

(8.2)

\mathbf{Ob}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))=\bigvee_{b}\mathbf{Ob}{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(b)),

where
b
ranges over all objects of
{\eus{C}}
.

The action of
{\eus{C}}
on
{\mathcal{X}}
defines a morphism

(8.3)

\theta\colon\mathbf{Ob}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(c)),

and this allows us to define the

\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-object
of morphisms of
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c)
as the pullback displayed in the diagram

(8.4)

\textstyle{\textbf{Mor}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T,S)}
\textstyle{\textbf{Mor}({\mathcal{X}}(c))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T,S)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Ob}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\times\mathbf{Ob}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta\times\theta}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(c))\times\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(c)).}

Composition is induced by composition in
{\mathcal{X}}(c)
.

To describe the pullback more explicitly, writing elementwise for the sake of exposition, let

(f,x)\in\mathbf{Ob}{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(b))\ \ \text{and}\ \ (f^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in\mathbf{Ob}{\eus{C}}(b^{\prime},c)\wedge\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(b^{\prime})).

Then

\textbf{Mor}(\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\Big((f,x),(f^{\prime},x^{\prime})\Big)=\textbf{Mor}({\mathcal{X}}(c))\Big(\theta(f,x),\theta(f^{\prime},x^{\prime})\Big).

The action map

\mathrm{St}\theta\colon{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(b)\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c)

descends to the smash product from the map on the product given on objects by
(\mathrm{St}\theta)(h,(f,x))=(h\circ f,x)
for
h\in{\eus{C}}(b,c)
.

For a morphism
\alpha:(f,x)\longrightarrow(f^{\prime},x^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(b)
and
\lambda\colon h\longrightarrow h^{\prime}
in
{\eus{C}}(b,c)
, the corresponding morphism
\mathrm{St}\theta(h,(f,x))\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}\theta(h^{\prime},(f^{\prime},x^{\prime}))
is defined to be

\theta(h\circ f,x)\xrightarrow{\varphi^{-1}}\theta(h,\theta(f,x))\xrightarrow{\theta(\lambda,\alpha)}\theta(h^{\prime},\theta(f^{\prime},x^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{\varphi}\theta(h^{\prime}\circ f,x).

Then
\mathrm{St}\theta
gives
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}
a strict
{\eus{C}}
-algebra structure by the strict functoriality of composition in
{\eus{C}}
.

For a
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphism
\textstyle{(F,\delta)\colon{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}
,
we define
\mathrm{St}(F,\delta)\colon\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}
by letting
\mathrm{St}(F,\delta)_{c}:\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{X}}(c)\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
be the functor sending
(f,x)
to
(f,Fx)
and
\alpha\colon(f,x)\longrightarrow(g,y)
to the composite

\theta_{Y}(f,Fx)\xrightarrow{\delta^{-1}}F(\theta_{X}(f,x))\xrightarrow{F\alpha}F(\theta_{X}(g,y))\xrightarrow{\delta}\theta_{Y}(g,Fy).

It is straightforward to check that these form the components of a strict
{\eus{C}}
-morphism.
We can similarly define the action of
\mathrm{St}
on
{\eus{C}}
-transformations.

For a weak
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra
({\mathcal{Y}},\theta,\varphi)
,
we define
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphisms

\textstyle{i\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\,\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}}

and

\textstyle{m\colon\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\,{\mathcal{Y}}}

as follows. The component
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-functor
i_{c}\colon{\mathcal{Y}}(c)\longrightarrow\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
is given by
i_{c}(y)=(\mathrm{id}_{c},y)
on objects
y
of
{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
and
i_{c}(\gamma)=\gamma
on morphisms
\gamma
of
{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
. The latter makes sense since
\theta(\mathrm{id}_{c},y)=y
. The component of the pseudonaturality
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-transformation

\textstyle{{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge{\mathcal{Y}}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\wedge i_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip 8.19447pti_{b,c}}
\textstyle{{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{St}\theta}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}(c)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{c}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)}

at
(f,y)
is the morphism
(f,y)\longrightarrow(\mathrm{id}_{c},\theta(f,y))
in
\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
corresponding to the identity map of
\theta(f,y)
in
{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
.

The component
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-functors
m_{c}\colon\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
are given by
(8.3)
on the
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-object of objects and by the top horizontal arrow in
(8.4)
on the
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-object of morphisms. The pseudonaturality constraint

\textstyle{{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\wedge m_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{St}\theta}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\hskip 4.09723pt\tilde{\varphi}}
\textstyle{{\eus{C}}(b,c)\wedge{\mathcal{Y}}(b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m_{c}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}(c)}

is induced by the invertible
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-transformation
\varphi
that witnesses the
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra structure of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

As is easily checked directly,
i
and
m
are inverse equivalences in
{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}
, so in particular,
i
is a level equivalence. The map
i
is 2-natural with respect to
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphisms and
{\eus{C}}
-transformations and is the unit of the adjunction. If
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a strict
{\eus{C}}
-algebra and thus of the form
{\mathcal{Y}}=\mathbb{J}{\mathcal{X}}
,
then
m
is
a strict
{\eus{C}}
-morphism, and moreover, it is 2-natural with respect to strict
{\eus{C}}
-morphisms and
{\eus{C}}
-transformations; it is the counit of the adjunction.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

The
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphism
\textstyle{m\colon\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\,{\mathcal{Y}}}
of the proof of
Theorem 8.1
is not strictly natural with respect to
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphisms of weak
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebras. With slightly extra work, one can prove that it gives the components of a pseudonatural transformation
m\colon\mathbb{J}\mathrm{St}\Longrightarrow\mathrm{id}
of endo-2-functors of
{\eus{C}}\text{-}{\bf{PsAlg}}
. As it is not necessary for our work, we shall not pursue this route. However, we will use the fact, noted in the proof, that
m_{c}\colon\mathrm{St}{\mathcal{Y}}(c)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
is an equivalence of
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-categories.

Recall
Notation 4.28
. We shall apply the following result to an iterated monoidal product
{\eus{C}}^{\wedge k}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}
in
Section 8.2
and to
\xi_{G}\colon{\mathscr{D}}_{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{F}_{G}
in
Section 10.2
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
{\eus{D}}
and
{\eus{C}}
be
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-categories and let
\xi\colon{\eus{D}}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}
be a
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-functor. Given a

{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra
{\mathcal{Y}}
,
there is a
{\eus{D}}
-morphism

\psi=\psi_{\xi}\colon\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}(\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\longrightarrow\xi^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})

that is 2-natural with respect to
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphisms and
{\eus{C}}
-transformations. Moreover, the diagram

\textstyle{\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{{\eus{D}}}}
\scriptstyle{\xi^{*}i_{{\eus{C}}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}(\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\xi^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})}

commutes, hence
\psi
is a levelwise equivalence.

Proof.

We can specify
\psi
as the
{\eus{D}}
-morphism corresponding to the
{\eus{D}}
-pseudomorphism

\xi^{*}i_{\eus{C}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.01387pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.01387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\colon\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.01387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawsquiggles@}}{\hbox{\kern 36.01387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\xi^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

under the adjunction of
Theorem 8.1
.
The claim about 2-naturality then follows from the 2-naturality of
i
. The commutativity of the diagram can be verified directly from the definition.
∎

Remark 8.7
.

For later use, we give an explicit description of
\psi
in terms of elements. For an object
d\in{\eus{D}}
,
\psi_{d}\colon\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}(\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})(d)\longrightarrow\xi^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})(d)
sends an object
(f,y)
, with
f\colon d^{\prime}\to d
in
{\eus{D}}
and
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\xi(d^{\prime}))
, to the object
(\xi(f),y)
.

The following lemma records the compatibility of the morphism
\psi
of
Lemma 8.6
with composition of
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-functors; it follows directly from the definitions.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
\nu\colon{\eus{E}}\longrightarrow{\eus{D}}
and
\xi\colon{\eus{D}}\longrightarrow{\eus{C}}
be
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-functors. Then the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{\eus{E}}((\xi\nu)^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{(\xi\nu)}}
\textstyle{(\xi\nu)^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{\eus{E}}(\nu^{*}\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\nu}}
\textstyle{\nu^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}(\xi^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{*}\psi_{\xi}}
\textstyle{\nu^{*}\xi^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{Y}})}

commutes for every
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

The following observation about the interaction of strictification with the external smash product (
Definition 4.29
) generalizes
[
32
, Lemma 3.5]
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let
{\eus{C}}
and
{\eus{D}}
be
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-2-categories,
{\mathcal{X}}
a
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra and
{\mathcal{Y}}
a
{\eus{D}}
-pseudoalgebra. Then there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}({\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\cong(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}})\,\overline{\wedge}\,(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}}),

which is
2
-natural with respect to the respective pseudomorphisms and pseudotransformations.
In particular, up to composing with this canonical isomorphism, for a
{\eus{C}}
-pseudomorphism
\textstyle{E\colon{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}
and an

{\eus{D}}
-pseudomorphism
\textstyle{F\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}}
, the
{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}
-morphism
\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}(E\,\overline{\wedge}\,F)
corresponds to
(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}E)\,\overline{\wedge}\,(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}F)
.

Proof.

On the level of objects, the identification
follows from
(2.8)
and
(8.2)
.
Again writing elementwise,
it is just the twist that sends an object
((e,f),(x,y))
of
\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}({\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,{\mathcal{Y}})(c,d)
to the object

((e,x),(f,y))
of
(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}})(c)\wedge(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}})(d)
.

Note that since the
\mathcal{V}_{\ast}
-functor

m_{\eus{C}}\wedge m_{\eus{D}}\colon(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\wedge(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}}(d))\to{\mathcal{X}}(c)\wedge{\mathcal{Y}}(d)

is an equivalence (
Remark 8.5
), it is fully faithful
[
44
, Lemma 4.17]
,
in the sense that the diagram

\textstyle{\textbf{Mor}\Big((\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\wedge(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}}(d))\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T,S)}
\textstyle{\textbf{Mor}({\mathcal{X}}(c)\wedge{\mathcal{Y}}(d))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T,S)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Ob}\Big((\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}}(c))\wedge(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}}(d))\Big)^{\times 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Ob}({\mathcal{X}}(c)\wedge{\mathcal{Y}}(d))^{\times 2}}

is a pullback square which is isomorphic to the pullback square in
(8.4)
that defines

\textbf{Mor}(\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}({\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,{\mathcal{Y}}))
.
This shows that the
\mathcal{V}_{*}
-categories
\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}(X\overline{\wedge}Y)(c,d)
and
(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}X)(c)\wedge(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}Y)(d)
are isomorphic. One can check that these isomorphisms respect the action of
{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}
, thus proving the result.
∎

We record the relationship between the 2-natural transformation
\psi
of
Lemma 8.6
and the canonical isomorphism
of
Lemma 8.9
.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
{\eus{C}}
,
{\eus{C}}^{\prime}
,
{\eus{D}}
, and
{\eus{D}}^{\prime}
, be

and

be

-2-functors, and let

{\mathcal{X}}
be a
{\eus{C}}
-pseudoalgebra and
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a
{\eus{D}}
-pseudoalgebra.
Then the following diagram commutes, where the unnamed isomorphisms are those of
Lemma 8.9
.

\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}^{\prime}\wedge{\eus{D}}^{\prime}}\Big((\xi\wedge\zeta)^{*}({\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{\xi\wedge\zeta}}
\textstyle{(\xi\wedge\zeta)^{*}\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}\wedge{\eus{D}}}({\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}^{\prime}\wedge{\eus{D}}^{\prime}}\Big((\xi^{*}{\mathcal{X}})\,\overline{\wedge}\,(\zeta^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\Big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{(\xi\wedge\zeta)^{*}(\mathrm{St}_{\eus{C}}{\mathcal{X}}\,\overline{\wedge}\,\mathrm{St}_{\eus{D}}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{C}}^{\prime}}\xi^{*}{\mathcal{X}})\,\overline{\wedge}\,(\mathrm{St}_{{\eus{D}}^{\prime}}\zeta^{*}{\mathcal{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
